### GATE-B-CLONED-COORDINATE (2026-08-27) — THE CERTIFIED GROWTH CONSTANT IMPROVES BY 75/64 BECAUSE SEPARATION IS FREE, AND THE FLOAT CENSUS IS SHOWN TO MISS WITNESSES AS WELL AS INVENT THEM

* **No lemma uses separation (PROVED by reading the proof).** `PROOF.md`
  Proposition 16. Lemma 1 needs only that the two block join events are
  independent under independent uniform product rows; Lemmas 2 and 3 need only
  block independence and that the four transition probabilities sum to one; the
  additivity corollary needs only \(|\mathcal H|=|\mathcal F||\mathcal G|\).
  Separation appears once, where it is *concluded* for the powers of a
  normalized base, never assumed.
* **One base-level Reimer check certifies every power (PROVED).** Reimer for
  \(\mathcal F^{\boxtimes k}\) asks \(kIm^{k-1}\ge\lceil km^k\log_2m/2\rceil\);
  an integer dominating a real dominates its ceiling, so it reduces to
  \(2I\ge m\log_2m\), i.e. \(m^m\le2^{2I}\), i.e. exactly \(I\ge R_m\). A first
  attempt wrote this as \(m^{2I}<2^{4I}\), which is false for \(m>4\); the audit
  returned `FAILED` and caught it. Corrected witness \(70^{70}\le2^{448}\)
  (\(\log_2=429.05\) against \(448\)).
* **Growth constant improved (CERTIFIED).** `n8clone_hi`, a 70-row \(n=8\)
  family whose coordinates 0 and 1 coincide, has defect \(317/490\) and
  \(A_+\le-3/80\), giving asymptotic slope \(3/640=0.0046875\) against the
  published \(1/250=0.004\) — improvement exactly \(75/64\). Powers audited by
  direct instantiation (`audit_clone_power.py`, verdict
  `EVERY_POWER_ADMISSIBLE`; the 4,900-row square rebuilt from its rows has
  defect \(210171/240100=1-(173/490)^2\) exactly). With the size ceiling,
  \((3/640)n-3/80\le\Lambda(n)\le4n/5\).
* **The mechanism is feasibility, not structure.** Cloning a coordinate changes
  neither size, defect, nor join structure; it only adds incidence. Size 70 is
  impossible at \(n=7\), where \(7\cdot28=196<R_{70}=215\). The eighth
  coordinate is spent entirely on clearing Reimer, and the reward is a base
  whose \(-A_+\) per coordinate beats anything available at seven. The cost is
  separation, which the definitions do not charge for.
* **\(S_3\times S_5\) class complete (CERTIFIED).** 16,777,215 masks, 13,470
  canonical families, 54 float-negative, 52 certified negative. Its best,
  `n8max`, sits at the *maximum* admissible size \(m=80\) with incidence
  \(256=8\cdot32\), the largest any \(n=8\) family can carry: defect
  \(2343/3200\), ratio \(\ge2432/58575=0.0415194\), the best among separating
  families. \(k=3\) moved neither the defect frontier nor the slope.
* **The float evaluator fails in both directions (verified).** Three of the 54
  \(k=3\) float negatives are exactly non-negative, and one family the census
  called non-negative is exactly **negative** (defect \(94/125\), float
  \(+0.00003418\), exact \(-0.00005157\)) — a *missed* witness. Largest
  float/exact gap \(3.24\times10^{-3}\), above \(k=2\)'s \(2.03\times10^{-3}\).
  This is why the re-ranking threshold is \(+0.01\) rather than \(0\).
* **Cost.** 77 tests pass. One worker per job, `nice -n 19`, machine CPU under
  25%. New artifacts: `uc/gate_b/audit_clone_power.py`, the clone and `n8max`
  certificates, the \(k=3\) census and exact frontier, `ClonedCoordinateBase`.

### E389-MASK-POISSON-REDUCTION (2026-08-28) - THE DOUBLE MASK IS CARRIED EXACTLY THROUGH PASCADI'S FIRST DISPERSION SUM; ITS MASS STAYS SMALL BUT ITS MOVING CENTER EXPANDS THE OFF-DIAGONAL SUPPORT FROM $x^{1/2}$ TO $x^{7/8}$

* **Theorem 23.1 (PROVED).** For $(k,r)=1$, $Kk\equiv1\pmod{r^2}$ and
  $r$-periodic $W$,
  \[
  \sum_{mk\equiv-1(r)}f(m)W((mk+1)/r)
  =\frac1r\sum_j\widehat f(j/r^2)e_{r^2}(-Kj)
  \widehat W(-jK\bmod r).
  \]
  The proof is one Fourier expansion and Poisson summation; the map
  $(a,h)\mapsto j=rh-ak$ is bijective.
* **The paired first-dispersion identity is also exact.** For
  $k_1\equiv k_2\pmod r$, the mask product has Poisson center
  $(ak_1-bk_2)/r$ and phase
  $e_{r^2}(a-b)e_r(-h\overline{k_1})$. The artifact checks the complete modular
  reindexing and phase identity on a nontrivial finite modulus.
* **Dual mass is not the blocker (PROVED).** Each mask frequency repeats
  $O(H)$ times. Fourier $\ell^1$ remains
  $O(H\log p_1\log p_2)$ and Fourier $\ell^2$ mass remains $O(H)$ by Parseval.
* **Exact primary-proof exponents.** Pascadi has
  $\mathcal M=\mathcal L=x^{3/8}$,
  $\mathcal N=H=x^{1/4}$, $K=R=x^{5/8}$. The spectral level and native
  off-diagonal support are $x^{1/2}$; the mask center makes the support
  $x^{7/8}$. The old $1/16$ mask-period comparison is only pre-Poisson.
  Post-Cauchy inflation is $3/8$; benchmarking against the native $1/4$
  additive saving leaves $1/8$.
* **Additive reciprocity (PROVED, but no closure).**
  $e_{r^2}(-Kj)=e_k(j\overline{r^2})e(-j/(kr^2))$. It trades the square
  conductor for an inverse-square trace in the semiprime $r=p_1p_2$ modulo
  the smooth composite $k$. Drappeau--Shparlinski's nonlinear theorem requires
  prime modulus.
* **Square-moduli large sieves (FAILED).** Even an optimistic one-sided
  projection gives Baier--Zhao $\Delta=x^{9/8+o(1)}$ and Cauchy error
  $x^{17/16+o(1)}$; Zhao's conjectural bound reaches only order $x$. The
  bilinear semiprime-square family gives $x^{23/16+o(1)}$, excess $7/16$,
  even under the generic conjectural bound.
* **Unbalanced $P_2$ (FAILED SUBSTITUTE).** If $w+1=brs$, $r\ge s$, the large
  factor is a short-cofactor prime exactly when $r>2bs$; standard Chen gives no
  such ratio. In Input VII that inequality disqualifies the candidate, while
  its failure adds two digit masks in place of one and retains the $5/4$ lift.
* **Exact external theorem.** Equations (93)--(94) state the paired
  moving-center extension of Pascadi's `lem:expo-bound-convo`, plus its
  one-mask cross variant, at the exact target ranges. It reduces to Pascadi's
  lemma for $W=1$ and is sufficient for (74). No matching theorem was located
  through August 2026.
* **Verification.** 140 tests pass. New direct regressions certify the complete
  modular reindexing, paired phase cancellation, additive reciprocity, the
  $1/2\to7/8$ and lowered-level exponent maps, square-sieve arithmetic, and the
  $P_2$ dichotomy. Both producers are deterministic after removing runtime
  metadata and pin every code input by SHA-256.
* **Costs.** Threshold producer 2.749 s, smooth-pair producer 5.001 s, suite
  3.770 s. One process each under `nice 10`; post-run CPU
  18.40% user + 3.21% system.
* **Artifacts.** `smooth_pair_transfer.py`,
  `data/smooth_pair_transfer.json`, THEOREMS.md Section 23,
  LOCALIZATION Input VII, README, root project index, and focused tests.


### E389-MIXED-LARGE-SIEVE-BARRIER (2026-08-28) - THE DOUBLE MASK IS EXPANDED EXACTLY; ZERO FREQUENCY IS CLOSED, ONE-SIDED LIFTS STOP AT ORDER $x$, AND THE BILINEAR TERM IS PRECISELY PASCADI'S EXPLICITLY OPEN MIXED LARGE-SIEVE SEQUENCE

* **Lemma 20.1 (PROVED).** The mask has exactly four frequency classes. With
  \(M=p_1p_2\), the genuinely bilinear phase is
  \(e_M((h_1p_2^2+h_2p_1^2)\ell)\); every \(h_1h_2\ne0\) numerator is primitive
  modulo \(M\). One-sided periods are \(p_1,p_2\), bilinear period \(M\).
* **A modulus-bookkeeping error is corrected inline.** Limiting exponent
  \(15/16\) belongs only to one-sided lifts
  \(p_1^2p_2,p_1p_2^2\). The genuinely bilinear character is primitive modulo
  \(M^2\) in \(n\), limiting exponent \(5/4\), although it remains modulus
  \(M=x^{5/8+o(1)}\) in the factorized quotient \(\ell\).
* **Lemma 20.2 (PROVED).** The quotient character has the exact lift
  \[
  \mathbf1_{s\equiv-1(M)}e_M(a(s+1)/M)
  =M^{-1}\sum_{j(M)}e_{M^2}((a+jM)(s+1)),
  \]
  and all \(M\) lifted frequencies are primitive. This is the exact step where
  black-box Pascadi stops applying.
* **Primary-proof fit audit (FAILED AS BLACK BOX).** Pascadi's smooth theorem
  allows arbitrary scalar outer weights; triply well-factorable weights belong
  to his prime theorem. The nonzero mask depends jointly on the factorization
  \(M=p_1p_2\) and on \((n+1)/M\), so it is neither a scalar modulus weight nor
  an inner coefficient independent of \(M\). `prop:triple-convo` itself forces
  \(R\le x^{5/8-o(1)}\).
* **Drappeau--Shparlinski (PARTIAL).** One-sided generic lifts save \(1/32\)
  individually but naive pair summation loses \(19/32\). The bilinear lift is
  outside the \(n\)-range, and in \(\ell\) its weight is shifted-smooth rather
  than smooth. At exact parameters: one-sided lift \(1121/1200\), saving
  \(79/2400\), loss \(283/480\); bilinear lift \(249/200>1\).
* **Parseval (FAILED APPROACH).** One-sided terms reach order \(x\), not
  \(o(x)\). Bilinear Parseval gives \(x^{9/8+o(1)}\), worse than the trivial
  order-\(x\) bound. It provides no weaker breakthrough.
* **Exact remaining estimate.** With shifted-smooth Fourier sums
  \(\mathcal S_M(a)\), equation (67b) is exactly
  \(\mathcal E_{10}+\mathcal E_{01}+\mathcal E_{11}=o(x)\), equation (74).
  The \(1/4\) versus \(5/16\) comparison gives a pre-Poisson gap \(1/16\);
  the preceding ledger entry supersedes it with the exact paired support map
  \(1/2\to7/8\) and theorem (94).
* **External blocker identified in Pascadi's own TeX.** After Cauchy, the mask
  generates coefficients \(hk\lambda-h'k'\lambda'\). Pascadi explicitly says
  no corresponding exceptional-spectrum large-sieve inequality is known for
  this mixed additive/multiplicative sequence. A search through August 2026
  found no unconditional successor; Baier arXiv:2503.18009 is conditional on
  additive-energy hypotheses.
* **Tail (PARTLY PROVED).** Repeated selected primes cost
  \(x^{11/16+o(1)}\). Repeated factors \(q>x^\zeta\) are \(o(x)\), with exact
  artifact floor exponent \(1949/2400\). Factors of \(\ell\), ultra-small
  factors of \(n\), and small repeated primes remain a separate open tail.
* **Fallbacks (PROVED LIMITS).** Any congruence forcing the first digit must
  contain \(p^2\); size forcing yields only \(O(x/\log x)\) candidates; splitting
  the selected modulus among fixed \(k\) primes leaves loss
  \(7/16+5/(16k)\) and the same bilinear \(5/4\) lift. Near-diagonal prime pairs
  can make the unweighted mask density tend to zero, but discarding
  \(p_2-p_1\le x^{5/16-\kappa}\) costs only
  \(x^{1+3\delta-\kappa+o(1)}=o(x)\) for \(\kappa>3\delta\).
* **Verification.** 136 tests pass. The producer and artifact certify the exact
  frequency partition, primitive $M^2$ lift, theorem-fit verdicts, all limiting
  and $\delta=1/1200$ exponents, failed Parseval boundary, selected/large-repeat
  tails, minimum $p^2$ congruence, fixed-$k$ splits, and near-diagonal cutoff.
  Both producers are deterministic after removing runtime metadata and pin all
  code inputs by SHA-256.
* **Costs.** Threshold producer 2.763 s, smooth-pair producer 5.018 s, suite
  3.764 s. One process each under `nice 10`; post-run CPU
  23.13% user + 2.99% system.
* **Artifacts.** `smooth_pair_transfer.py`,
  `data/smooth_pair_transfer.json`, THEOREMS.md Sections 20--22,
  LOCALIZATION inputs VI--VII, README and focused tests.


### E389-TYPE-II-SMOOTH-PAIR (2026-08-28) - PASCADI--YANG GIVES A POSITIVE-DENSITY CONSECUTIVE $3/8$-SMOOTH FAMILY, DISCHARGING THE SHORT-COFACTOR PAIR; R(2) IS NOW A STRUCTURED $q$-ADIC HALF-LIFT PROBLEM

* **A claimed barrier was false and is retracted in place.** The equation
  \(pa+1=p'b\) is parity-blocked, but it parameterizes the large-prime
  intersection, not the only route to the event. Yang's Theorem 1.5
  (arXiv:2607.16032) already proves a positive density of consecutive
  \(41/107+\varepsilon\)-smooth integers by counting the smooth complement.
  Section 18.4 and LOCALIZATION input V now say exactly that.
* **Theorem 19.1 (PROVED FROM A CITED THEOREM).** Pascadi's Theorem 1.5
  (arXiv:2505.00653v2) gives absolute smooth-number Bombieri--Vinogradov through
  \(x^{5/8-\varepsilon}\). Substituting
  \(5/16+5/16=5/8\) in Yang's printed two-prime proof gives
  \[
  \#\{n<x:P^+(n)<P^+(n+1)<x^{3/8+\varepsilon}\}\gg_\varepsilon x.
  \]
  This improves \(41/107\) to \(3/8\). The proof tracks disjoint prime ranges,
  a factor-two representation bound, Pascadi's summed error, and the positive
  Mertens--Dickman main term; it was not found stated in either source.
* **Consequence (PROVED).** After discarding \(o(x)\) small \(n\), both largest
  prime squares are below twice their terms. A positive lower density of
  consecutive pairs is therefore short-cofactor-free at both coordinates.
  Input V is **DISCHARGED** and the strict Bonferroni inequality (56) follows
  unconditionally. This still does not prove R(2): the level class \(B\)
  remains.
* **Lemma 19.2 (PROVED, elementary).** The centered half-lift set
  \(\mathcal H_q\subset\mathbb Z/q^2\mathbb Z\) has normalized Fourier
  \(\ell^1\)-norm at most \(H_{(q-1)/2}\le1+\log((q-1)/2)\). Hence the selected
  double mask costs only \(O(\log p_1\log p_2)\); Fourier complexity is not the
  blocker.
* **CORRECTED BY THE NEXT ENTRY.** The first draft priced the whole double mask
  at lift exponent \(15/16\). The exact centered expansion shows that value
  belongs only to the two one-sided frequency classes; genuinely bilinear
  frequencies are primitive modulo \(p_1p_2\) in \(\ell\) and lift to
  \((p_1p_2)^2\), limiting exponent \(5/4\), in \(n\). Equation (74), not the
  undifferentiated \(19/32\) estimate, is the exact Input VI.
* **Finite evidence, labelled.** At \(10^6\le n<1.5\cdot10^6\), exactly
  \(427/2{,}938=0.145337\) consecutive \(3/8\)-smooth pairs survive every level
  condition; full-block density \(0.000854\). A friendly-width Yang-shaped
  probe has 164 representations, 39 selected-prime passes
  (\(0.237805\) versus \(1/4\)), 25 fully good representations, and 12 satisfying
  both. Those widths are not theorem parameters.
* **Exact parameter certificate.** At $\varepsilon=1/200$,
  $\delta=1/1200$: selected modulus
  $[x^{149/240},x^{249/400}]<x^{187/300}$, cofactor
  $x^{91/240}<x^{19/50}$, largest one-sided lift $1121/1200$, individual
  one-sided saving $79/2400$, and naive one-sided excess $283/480$.
* **Verification.** 129 tests pass. `run_threshold.py` and
  `smooth_pair_transfer.py` are deterministic after removing runtime metadata;
  both artifacts pin every code input by SHA-256. Direct failure-regime tests
  cover the collapse below $\sqrt{2w}$, the $x=2$ shifted-square boundary,
  unsupported $\alpha$, exact rational powers, the Fourier harmonic bound, and
  all exponent identities.
* **Costs.** Threshold producer 2.711 s, smooth-pair producer 4.948 s, suite
  3.791 s. One process each under `nice 10`; post-run CPU
  20.78% user + 2.46% system.
* **Artifacts.** `run_threshold.py`, `smooth_pair_transfer.py`,
  `data/run_threshold.json`, `data/smooth_pair_transfer.json`, THEOREMS.md
  Sections 18--19, and LOCALIZATION inputs V--VI.


### E389-R2-LOCATED (2026-08-28) - FOUR NATURAL ROUTES TO R(2) ARE NOW SEPARATED BY EXACT OBSTRUCTIONS; THE SHIFTED-SQUARE FAMILY FREES BOTH SHORT-COFACTOR COORDINATES, BUT NO UNCONDITIONAL R(2) CLAIM IS MADE

* **Theorem 18.2 (PROVED, elementary).** For
  \(m=\lfloor(2X)^\alpha\rfloor\), \(\alpha>\tfrac12\), goodness collapses to
  smoothness, and a run of \(L\) consecutive \(m\)-good integers has lower
  density at least \(1-L\log(1/\alpha)\). Its positivity threshold is
  \(\alpha>e^{-1/L}\), \(0.606531\) at \(L=2\). Four finite blocks are
  consistency checks, not proof inputs.
* **Lemma 18.5 (PROVED, elementary).** For every \(x\ge3\), \(x^2-1\) and \(x^2\)
  are both short-cofactor-free. Hence at least
  \(\lfloor\sqrt W\rfloor-2\) integers \(w\le W\) have both \(w,w+1\) free of
  the failure class costing \(\log2\) per coordinate. Within this construction
  only the level conditions remain.
* **SUPERSEDED IN THE SAME SESSION - direct prime--prime sieve (18.4).** The
  parameterization \(pa+1=p'b\) is genuinely binary and GRH does not remove its
  parity obstruction. The conclusion that this blocked the event was false:
  the next ledger entry records the Type-I/Type-II smooth-complement proof and
  retracts the claim.
* **FAILED APPROACH - shifted-square residue density (18.6).** At fixed band
  \(r\), only \(O(X/q)\) parameters carry \(q\), against a required period
  \(q^r\); their ratio is
  \(O(X/q^{r+1})=O(X^{-r/(r+2)})\), and can be \(O(X^{-1})\). For
  \(x\in[X,2X)\), every prime \(q\in(X,\sqrt2X]\) gives a failed local \(q\)-test
  at \(x=q+1\) and a passed one at \(x=q-1\): exactly \(29{,}610\) and \(0\)
  failures at \(X=10^6\). On \(10^6\le x<1.03\cdot10^6\), the measured good
  densities are \(0.39123\), \(0.07183\), and \(0.02553\) for \(x^2-1\), \(x^2\),
  and both. This is finite evidence, not R(2).
* **FAILED APPROACH - second moments (Proposition 18.3).** Exact rational joint
  laws have marginal \(31/100>1-\log2\) in every coordinate and zero full-run
  mass for \(L=2,3,4\). At \(L=2\),
  \(E[(G_0+G_1)^2]=E[G_0+G_1]+2E[G_0G_1]\): the second-moment input strong enough
  to force a pair is exactly the missing pair correlation.
* **FAILED APPROACH - attack the long run at once (Theorem 18.9).** Every
  witness satisfies the new size floor \(k^2>(m+1)\log2\). The positive
  parameter range of Theorem 18.2 lies strictly below it for every \(m\ge3\).
  Exact rational log/exp intervals check all \(3\le m\le2047\), with minimum
  log-margin \(0.016972\) at \(m=4\); an elementary monotonicity argument
  continues from \(m=2048\). Choosing \(m\) in an arithmetic progression or
  near a primorial cannot change this run-layer result.
* **The deficit jump.** The first union bound misses by \(0.015668\) at \(L=1\)
  and \(1.031336\) at \(L=2\), a factor \(65.825\). Section 17 supplied
  \(0.045021\) of diagonal overlap; cross-coordinate input is indispensable.
* **Corrections before checkpoint.** The initial draft overstated (58) as a
  uniform factor-\(X\) shortage; the exact loss ranges from
  \(X^{r/(r+2)}\) to \(X\). It also called a \(0.02553\) finite block density
  ``R(2) true'' and cited unpinned \(10^7\) numbers. Those claims are removed.
  The artifact-backed values above are the only ones retained.
* **Implementation.** `run_threshold.py` and `data/run_threshold.json`; exact
  rational enclosures, no floating-point proof boundary. A duplicated \(C_p\)
  helper was removed in favour of the repository SSOT `compensation_count`.


### GATE-B-EIGHTH-COORDINATE (2026-08-27) — THE NUMERATOR'S GROWTH LAW IS CLOSED AT $\Theta(n)$, THE LOCAL FRONTIER MOVES FROM 0.6598 TO 0.5673, AND THE FIRST FAMILY BEATS THE PUBLISHED REPAIR RATIO

* **Size ceiling (PROVED, exact).** `uc/gate_b/PROOF.md` Lemma 9. Incidence is
  at most \(n\lfloor2m/5\rfloor\) and Reimer demands at least \(m\log_2m/2\), so
  every admissible family satisfies \(\log_2m\le4n/5\), tested exactly as the
  integer inequality \(m^5\le2^{4n}\). Verified for every admissible size at
  every \(3\le n\le16\). Close to sharp: the largest admissible size at
  \(n=11\) is 445, i.e. \(\log_2m=8.7977\) against \(8.8\). The repository had
  been carrying this inequality per base as the `reimer_witness` string without
  recognising it as a general ceiling.
* **Numerator growth closed (PROVED, two-sided).** Corollary 10. With
  \(Q,C_+\ge0\) the ceiling gives the first unconditional \(n\)-explicit bound
  \(-A_+\le4n/5\), and the certified powers give the matching linear lower
  bound, so \(\Lambda(n)=\Theta(n)\). Only the *denominator* of the repair ratio
  is still open.
* **Two defect floors (PROVED), one attained.** Lemma 11: the cap forces
  \(\mathbb E|X\vee Y|\ge\frac85\bar s\) while a successful union has size at
  most \(M\), so \(\varepsilon_\vee\ge(\frac85\bar s-M)/(n-M)\). Lemma 13:
  downset counting gives \(\varepsilon_\vee\ge1-m^{-2}\sum_AN(A)^2\), attained
  with slack exactly zero. Corollary 12: the ratio is \(O(n)\) without a
  dominant set, so the local regime **requires** a set of size
  \(\ge\frac85\bar s\ge\frac45\log_2m\). Exhaustive audit over all 366
  admissible families at \(n\le4\): zero violations.
* **The certified bases are maximal (PROVED).** Proposition 14. 25 and 45 are
  the largest admissible sizes at \(n=6,7\); all three bases attain them with
  every degree exactly at the cap; **no set can be added to any of them**, over
  all \(2^n\) candidates. The one amplification route that lowers the defect and
  raises \(\log_2m\) at once is therefore empty here. A greedy attempt found
  this first: every base reported "no admissible addition" at step 0.
* **The combinatorial floor was never the obstruction.** At \(n=7,m=45\)
  admissible families reach defect \(0.3417\) while the best negative sits at
  \(0.6598\), a gap of \(0.318\). Cause measured: at fixed \(m\), \(\log_2 45\)
  is fixed and \(Q\) rises as the defect falls — \(5.4474\), \(5.4758\),
  \(5.5608\) at defects \(0.79\), \(0.66\), \(0.34\) — crossing \(5.4919\).
  Negativity is won only at the maximum admissible size.
* **The eighth coordinate (CERTIFIED).** That diagnosis predicts the frontier
  moves when the ceiling moves. The untried \(S_2\times S_6\) class at \(n=8\)
  (21 cells, 2,097,151 masks, 2,272 canonical families, 419 s) then gave, after
  exact rational re-evaluation of all 36 candidates with float \(A_+<0.01\):
  \(e^\star\le1144/1875=0.6101\) separating and \(139/245=0.5673\) among all
  admissible; and repair ratio \(0.0370512>0.0362591\) separating,
  \(0.0580391\) among all admissible — **the first families in this repository
  to beat the published base's ratio.** Sizes 70 and 75 are infeasible at
  \(n=7\) (\(196<R_{70}=215\)); the eighth coordinate supplies the missing
  incidence and in the 70-row families is an exact duplicate of the first, so
  coordinate cloning is the mechanism Lemma 9 predicts.
* **A false negative caught, and the frozen-target gate observed working.** The
  80-row family at defect \(2553/3200\) reads \(-0.00026687\) in float64 and
  \(+0.000454098\) exactly. Float/exact gaps reach \(2\times10^{-3}\) in this
  class. Both frozen rational targets for the new bases were first written from
  float values and both were **rejected** by the verifier before correction.
  All 15 families with float \(A_+\in[0,0.01)\) are exactly non-negative.
* **Cost.** 66 tests pass. One worker per job, `nice -n 19`, total machine CPU
  measured at 19.7% of 28 cores. New artifacts:
  `uc/gate_b/bound_local_regime.py` (standalone, stdlib-only),
  `uc/gate_b/certify_class_negatives.py`, `uc/gate_b/CURRENT_STATUS.md`,
  the `n8_k2` census and its exact frontier report.
* **Still OPEN and Frankl-equivalent.** By Proposition 7, \(c^\star\) restricted
  to \(\le n\) coordinates is finite exactly when no admissible family has
  \(\varepsilon_\vee=0\) and \(A_+<0\); such a family is union-closed with every
  frequency \(\le2/5\), so proving finiteness for all \(n\) gives a \(2/5\)
  frequency bound, about \(0.018\) beyond the published \(0.381966\).

### E389-R1-PROVED (2026-08-28) - THE FIRST RUNG OF CONJECTURE R IS NOW A THEOREM, AND THE CLAIM THAT BLOCKED IT WAS MY OWN ARITHMETIC ERROR

* **Theorem 17.7 (PROVED, Mertens + Vinogradov).** The $m$-compensation-good
  integers have lower density at least \(0.0293\), uniformly for \(m\le x^{1/4}\)
  and hence for every \(m\). This is Conjecture R(1), recorded as OPEN since the
  project began.
* **The blocker was a conflation, not a barrier.** Section 13 classified every
  integer of a block and concluded "the overlap that would rescue the union
  bound does not exist". What it had refuted was the *independence* prediction
  \(0.071745\) for the level-only class. The union bound overshoots \(1\) by only
  \(0.015668\), and the overlap it measured in the same table was \(0.053415\) -
  already \(3.4\times\) enough. Both documents are corrected in place, with the
  retraction stated rather than quietly edited.
* **What made the lower bound available.** The exact identity
  \(\#\mathrm{good}=x-\#A-\#B+\#(A\cap B)\) needs the overlap bounded from
  *below*. Section 13 tried the smooth side, which needs smooth numbers in
  progressions to moduli near \(\sqrt X\) - correctly declined. Counting from the
  prime side instead, the free variable is the large prime \(p\) and the level
  test is a congruence to a single modulus \(q^r\le y^{1-\delta}\), so
  Vinogradov's bound applies per modulus. **No averaging, so no
  Bombieri-Vinogradov and no GRH.** Recorded in `LOCALIZATION.md` as input IV,
  the project's first DISCHARGED analytic input.
* **Numbers, all one-sided with explicit tails.** Deficit \(0.015668\); overlap
  \(\ge0.045021\) from two multiplicity-free bands (\(\lambda_1\ge0.030515\),
  \(\lambda_2\ge0.014505\)); exponent-\(e\ge2\) tail \(\le6\cdot10^{-6}\) at
  \(10^{40}\) and \(\to0\); certified density \(\ge0.029348\). Ratio of supply to
  demand \(2.87\).
* **Two of my own errors caught by the audit, before publication.** \(\lambda_1\)
  was first written \(\ge0.030518\), above its own certified value; and Section
  17.9 quoted the \(m=27\) overlap densities for an artifact generated at
  \(m=1\). Both fixed; the \(m=1\) overlap is larger, so the point strengthens.
* **Verification.** 103 tests pass (10 new), including a direct small-\(N\)/
  large-\(m\) corner test - the family must be empty when every band prime falls
  below \(m\), a regime no screened block reaches - and an exhaustive check that
  every family member lies in \(A\cap B\) and none is counted twice.
* **CORRECTED LATER IN THE SAME SESSION.** Under cross-coordinate independence,
  the depth-3 total is below one only for $\pi<0.007834$, while
  $\pi\ge0.045$. This proves that the *independent depth-3 substitution* fails;
  it does not prove a structural R(2) obstruction. Sections 19--20 count the
  consecutive smooth complement, discharge the short-cofactor pair, and move
  the actual gap to the mixed $q$-adic large-sieve Input VII. The old phrase
  ``the term that rescues one coordinate defeats two'' is retracted as a claim
  about R(2), though it remains an exact description of that failed model.
* **The reduction is now finitary (PROVED).** (32) is an explicit power, so
  \(\#(G_m\cap[1,X])>4\pi(m)mX^{1-c_m}\) at one scale gives a witness \(k\le X\).
  For \(m=27\) it is vacuous below \(10^{236.3}\) and useful past \(10^{1241.6}\);
  recorded with those numbers rather than as an unquantified improvement.
* **Costs.** Certificate 8.4 s, suite 3.3 s. One process, `nice 10`, CPU 23%.

### E389-ELEMENTARY-EVICTION (2026-08-27) — ONE THREE-LINE THEOREM SUPERSEDES BOTH THE LINNIK AND THE GRH ROUTES, PRICES THE WHOLE CONGRUENCE PROGRAMME, AND MOVES THE REMAINING GAP ONTO A SINGLE EFFECTIVE CONSTANT

* **The master theorem (PROVED, elementary).** `e389/THEOREMS.md` §15.
  Any arithmetic progression of length at least
  \(P_0=\min\{p:p^2>2\,\mathrm{top},\ p>m\}\) contains a non-witness, whatever
  its common difference. Proof: pick any window position, choose the index
  \(j\equiv-u_0q^{-1}\pmod p\) so that \(p\mid k+i\), and note
  \(p^2>2(k+i)\) is (25) failing. Bertrand's postulate and one modular
  inversion; **no prime is asked to lie in a progression, a short interval, or
  anywhere else.**
* **What it replaces.** Effective class eviction at \(k<4M^2\) versus the
  \(M^{5.18}\) that Linnik's least prime would give (Xylouris); block eviction
  at \(M\le\sqrt N/2\) versus \(M\le\sqrt N/\log^{2+}N\) under GRH — the
  conditional route is *weaker* than the unconditional one here, because at
  \(q\approx\sqrt N\) the GRH error \(\sqrt N\log^2N\) exceeds the main term
  \(\sqrt N/\log N\). Section 8's Dirichlet statement becomes effective.
* **The programme is priced, not just one class.** Any family of classes
  consisting entirely of witnesses inside \([N,2N)\) needs
  \(r\ge\frac12\delta\sqrt N\) members to cover a proportion \(\delta\): a
  finite congruence system cannot describe positive density, and the witness
  set contains no progression of length \(2\sqrt{2Y}(1+o(1))\) below \(Y\).
* **A defect my own exhaustive screen caught.** The first proof of (36) claimed
  \(p>m\) followed from \(p^2>2\,\mathrm{top}\). It does not — at \(m=5\),
  \(A=\{1,\dots,5\}\) the size condition offers \(p=5\), which (25) never
  reaches. Fixed in the statement, the code, and a regression test; found by
  the screen at exactly that pair, not by reading.
* **A second text-only defect, from an outside read.** Corollary 15.4(b)
  printed \(P_0(N,m)=\min\{p:p^2>4N+2m\}\), dropping the \(p>m\) clause that
  (25) requires and that (36) carries. When \(m^2>4N+2m\) the size condition
  alone offers a prime below \(m\) — at \(m=100\), \(N=1000\) it offers 67 —
  which (25) exempts, so the printed corollary was false in that regime. The
  producer was never wrong (`block_prime` delegates to `progression_prime`,
  which takes \(\max(\lfloor\sqrt{2\,\mathrm{top}}\rfloor,m)\)), and every
  screened rectangle has \(N\ge10^6\gg m^2/4\) where the clause is vacuous —
  so **no screen could have caught this one**. Statement, Corollary (c)'s length
  bound, and the admissible \(P\)-range of 15.6 corrected; two tests added that
  do reach the \(m\)-dominated regime.
* **Sharpness (PROVED).** If every prime factor of \(M\) is at most \(m\) and
  \(M^2\ge N\), every \(n\in[N,2N)\) divisible by \(M\) satisfies (25). So the
  single-position route is sharp to within a factor 2: proved to \(\sqrt N/2\),
  obstructed from \(\sqrt N\). Realised at \(M=4620\), \(N=10^7\).
* **Certified beyond the proof, twice.** A covering criterion decides in
  \(O(L\log L)\) whether one prime evicts *every* class; it certifies
  \(\theta=\log M/\log N\) to \(0.5451\) (\(m=13\)), \(0.5702\) (\(m=27\)),
  \(0.5926\) (\(m=51\)) at \(N=10^{12}\), within \(0.05\%\) of the measure
  ceiling \(L\lfloor N/P_0\rfloor\) that no covering argument can pass. An
  exhaustive census of \([10^7,2\cdot10^7)\) evicts every class of every tested
  modulus to \(\theta=0.990\) for \(m=27,51\).
* **The barrier is equidistribution, and it is measured.** Scanning primes past
  the proved threshold succeeds with mean cost \(MP_0/(LN)\): predicted
  \(2.26,\ 9.01,\ 35.9,\ 142.9\) primes at \(\theta=0.60,0.65,0.70,0.75\)
  against measured \(2.11,\ 8.81,\ 38.2,\ 157.5\). The residues \(p^{-1}\bmod
  M\) behave as uniform draws; there is no arithmetic conspiracy, only a bound
  to prove. Where a single prime fails, the cause is exact: the \(L\) targets
  collapse onto \(\approx s\) arcs when \(p^{-1}/M\) is near a rational with
  denominator \(s\), and the coverage predictor reproduces the measured success
  rates (0.75 predicted vs 0.695 measured at ratio 2, 0.35 vs 0.33 at 20).
* **The remaining gap is one constant (literature, verified at source).** Two
  independent librarian sweeps. Balog–Pomerance (*Proc. AMS* 115 (1992)) and
  Shiu (*J. reine angew. Math.* 313 (1980)) both bound survivors for **every
  individual modulus** in the open range with no averaging and no shape
  condition; the Dickman margin is \(1/\rho(2)=3.26\); and the subgroup
  mechanism that breaks per-class bounds cannot operate because
  \(y\approx q^{1+\delta}\) sits far above \(q^{1/(4\sqrt e)}\). Every
  Bombieri–Vinogradov-style result (Fouvry–Tenenbaum, Drappeau, Maynard,
  Pascadi \(x^{66/107}\)) is an average over moduli and is structurally unusable
  for one class; every individual-\(q\) smooth-number asymptotic (Granville,
  Soundararajan, Harper, Banks–Shparlinski) needs \(\log x/\log q\to\infty\)
  while ours is \(\approx2\). Recorded in `e389/LOCALIZATION.md` with hypotheses
  and verdicts.
* **Verification.** 91 tests pass (17 new, including the corrected \(p>m\)
  boundary, the covering measure identity against direct enumeration, and the
  smooth-modulus obstruction). Both new producers deterministic; artifacts
  `data/elementary_class_eviction.json`, `data/survivor_census.json`. A
  cosmetic rename of `_is_prime_64` was reverted rather than invalidating 33
  provenance-pinned artifacts for a style gain.
* **External blocker recorded.** The independent `fable` review of (36) and
  16.2 was rejected by the Claude CLI quota (`seven_day_overage_included`,
  `org_level_disabled`), twice. The proofs were instead checked against
  exhaustive screens, which is how the \(p>m\) defect was found; an outside
  read of Sections 15–16 is still owed.
* **Costs.** Eviction certificate 16 s, census 23 s, suite 2.7 s. Both producers
  reproduce their artifacts byte-identically from their documented default
  commands. One process, `nice 10`, machine below 25 %.


### LIU-H2-CHART-COVER (2026-08-28) - THE FACTOR OF EIGHT IS CLOSED, THE q RANGE WAS THE REAL GAP, AND THE AMBIENT NINE-VARIABLE EXTENSION IS REFUTED

* **What was asked.** Lift the certified smooth-chart radius from \(1/256\) to
  \(1/32\) by a radial quadrature, on the stated grounds that a uniform box
  cover cannot work because the mean-value form needs the whole segment from
  the origin enclosed.

* **Both grounds were wrong, and saying so is the result.** A cover suffices.
  PSD is a *pointwise* property, so certifying it on each cell of an exhaustive
  cover certifies it on the union; and the box is convex and contains the chart
  centre, so the mean-value step obtains its segments from geometry once rather
  than per cell. Per-cell segment containment would be needed only if
  positivity were certified from each cell's own local expansion, which is not
  what the pencil route does. No radial quadrature was built, because none is
  required.

* **The radius was not the binding gap.** Ingredient (iii) in
  `uc/liu9_qdegenerate.py` names `interior_q_min = 1/4096`, not \(1/4\); both
  the inherited certificate and the first cover attempt covered only
  \(q\in[1/4,3/4]\). A uniform \(q\) grid stops certifying below
  \(q=1/64\). Cause, measured: `gap.hdd` and `dist.hdd` both carry an exact
  factor \(q(1-q)\) -- the ratio \(m_{22}/(q(1-q))\) is \(0.69512040\) at
  \(q=1/4,1/64,1/128,1/1024,1/4096\) alike, to nine digits -- so the true
  entry vanishes linearly at the endpoints while a fixed-width cell's enclosure
  error does not. Octave cells of relative width \(1/64\) fix it, the same
  device `liu9_boundary_layer._octave_grid` already uses on the entropy side.

* **PROVED.** `uc/liu9_chart_cover.py`: the pencil is PSD on
  \(|s-x|,|d|\le1/32\) for every \(q\in[1/4096,4095/4096]\) at
  \(\kappa\ge4119063/33554432\), over \(393{,}216\) exhaustively abutting
  cells; weakest determinant margin \(2.48\times10^{-5}\) at
  \((s-x,d,q)=(15/512,-15/512,1/4096)\). Coverage is verified in exact
  `Fraction` arithmetic: endpoints land on the box boundary, consecutive cells
  abut with no gap and no slack, and the covered region is checked to contain
  the chart centre -- an annulus would pass every PSD test and prove nothing.

* **Step B's deficit is explicit, not zero.** At the chart centre both
  components collapse to \(\{x\!:\!m,\,0\!:\!1-m\}\), so every \(q\)
  cancels structurally and the centre gap is a \(q\)-free constant --
  computing it that way matters, because `gap_jet` at an *interval* \(q\)
  loses the cancellation to dependency and returns width \(\approx6\times
  10^{-3}\) around a constant. Carrying the collapse as a jet in \(s\) gives
  the centre value and \(s\)-gradient as \(q\)-free enclosures, and the
  \(d\)-gradient is exactly zero for every \(q\) because each term carries a
  \(q\)-free multiplier times \((1-q)(-q)+q(1-q)\), all of whose coefficients
  vanish in exact `Fraction` arithmetic. So the bound is an all-\(q\)
  enclosure, not a sampled one: Liu's \(x\) is a numerically determined root
  of (87)-(90), and the certified statement is
  \(\mathrm{gap}-\kappa\,\mathrm{dist}^2\ge-7.361\times10^{-69}\) for every
  \(q\) in the covered range, inherited unchanged from the \(1/256\)
  certificate.  Leaving \(\partial_s\) sampled would have left the dominant
  term of the deficit resting on a probe set.

* **REFUTED.** `uc/liu9_ninevar.py`: the chart result does not extend to an
  unrestricted ambient nine-variable neighbourhood. At
  \((y,\varepsilon,q)=(1/32,1/1024,1/2)\) the split
  \((p-\varepsilon,\varepsilon,q,x,y,0,x,y,0)\) gives raw gap
  \(-5.5338\times10^{-4}\), pencil \(-5.5348\times10^{-4}\); exact
  `Fraction` bounds put the linear coefficient below \(-1/2\) and the finite
  pencil below \(-1/2048\). Leading order \(\varepsilon\); the
  \(\varepsilon\log(1/\varepsilon)\) coefficient is exactly \(0\).

* **CONDITIONAL, and this is now the live gap.** That counterexample is
  mean-INFEASIBLE (mean-target \(-6.4408\times10^{-4}\)), so it does not
  refute Hypothesis 2, which lives in \(\{\mathrm{mean}\ge px\}\). The
  exact mean-preserving control at the same \(y\) has linear coefficient
  above \(1/64\); over a 65-point \(y\) scan the mean-preserving family is (**superseded 2026-08-29** by the universal enclosure in `uc/liu9_transverse.py`)
  positive at every point (0 negative) while the ambient split is negative at
  46. Weakest mean-preserving coefficient \(7.865\times10^{-8}\) at
  \(y=707/1024\approx x\), where the inserted atom merges with the existing
  one and the perturbation degenerates.

* **Superseded.** The exact second-order theorem now supplies that
  chart-to-nine-variable step and proves the local radius \(1/4096\). The
  unproved statement is global complement minimality, not the local tube.

* **A self-inflicted error worth recording.** My first independent check of the
  subagent's counterexample hand-built `_ScalarOps(mpmath.mpf, mpmath.mpf(1))`
  and got gap \(=-0.2326\) at the chart centre, where it must vanish -- the
  ad-hoc ops object was not binary entropy at all. The subagent was right and I
  was wrong; calibrating against a known zero before trusting a disagreement is
  what caught it. Re-verified with the sanctioned `gap_mp`: centre
  \(-7.8\times10^{-62}\), and every reported digit reproduced.

* **Verification.** 21 cover mutation tests, 8 nine-variable tests, and the
  five sibling suites all pass; `bridge_uc` prints `BRIDGE CHECK PASS`. Cover
  mutations reject an omitted endpoint cell, an interior gap, an inflated
  radius, a non-dividing cell width, a cover that excludes the chart centre, a

### LIU-H2-TRANSVERSE-POSITIVITY (2026-08-29) - A UNIVERSAL ENCLOSURE REPLACES A 65-POINT SCAN, AND THE ASYMMETRIC EXTENSION IS FREE

- **Attacked the named decisive question** -- whether a feasible-half-space
  nine-variable theorem holds -- rather than the volume around it.
- **Found the mechanism.** Liu's eq. (87) is the protocol identity
  \(\mathrm{prot}(x,x)=1-x^2\); entropy symmetry then collapses \(K(x,x)\) to
  \(h(x^2)\) for every \(\beta\). Consequence: the transverse coefficient has a
  **double root** at \(y=x\), which is why the old scan bottomed out at
  \(7.865\times10^{-8}\) near \(x\) -- it was sampling a double root.
- **Certified** \(L\ge-4.562\times10^{-136}\) on all of \((0,1)\) in five
  exactly abutting regions; the deficit is the *square* of Liu's root residual.
- **Extended for free** to the asymmetric two-support family via an exact
  decoupling identity.
- **Two errors caught by discipline, both mine.** (i) I claimed \(L'(x)=0\)
  holds for every \(\beta\); testing it showed it does not, and the \(\beta\)
  equation is load-bearing. (ii) I hand-built `_ScalarOps(mpmath.mpf, ...)`
  again -- the same trap as the previous session -- and got 586 spurious
  "negative" configurations. Calibrating against the known zero at \(y=x\)
  caught it; with the sanctioned `gap_mp` there are **zero** negatives. Both
  are now mutation tests.
- **Also caught:** the Arb enclosure of \(L(x)\) is \(2.7\times10^{-68}\) of
  *dependency error*, not a deficit; using it would have swamped the true bound
  by 68 orders. It is excluded and labelled NOT USED.
- **Novelty checked against the primary source**, not assumed: the paper states
  (87) directly, never writes the quartic, and never remarks the collapse; no
  citing work engages the optimizer's local structure.

### LIU-H2-PIECEWISE-LEMMA (2026-08-28) — THREE OF THE FOUR LOCAL INGREDIENTS ARE NOW PROVED, A UNITS ERROR IN THE ONLY COMPARISON THAT MATTERED IS FIXED, AND THE LAST BLOCKER IS LOCALIZED TO INTERVAL DEPENDENCY RATHER THAN GEOMETRY

* **A stratum nobody had named is closed (PROVED).** `uc/liu9_mirror_layer.py`.
  The face that ingredient (ii) reduces to is \(C^3\) only on
  \([y_0,1-y_0]\); \(h'''\) blows up at \(b_j\to1\) exactly as at 0, and

  infimum of \(g\) across the stratum, \(g(1-t_0)\), not by \(g(1)\).  Three
  percentages here are distinct and were briefly conflated: at \(t_0=1/32\)
  the unsquared cost \(b(b-x)\) is \(12.92\%\) smaller at the inner edge,
  \(g=[b(b-x)]^2\) is \(24.16\%\) smaller, and the critical radius, moving as
  \(\sqrt{g}\), would be overclaimed by \(14.83\%\) (\(0.17951\) against
  \(0.20613\)).  The divisor is \(g\), so \(24.16\%\) is the relevant one.  The monotonicity that puts the infimum
  at the inner edge was an unstated assumption until an external review
  flagged it; it is now certified by `certify_face_infimum` and guarded by a
  mutation test. The singular coefficient
  \(1-2(1-\beta)W_1-2\beta A_1\) is genuinely two-sided and the mean does
  not sign it. The *tube* does: \(W_1\le\rho^2/g(1-t_0)\),
  \(g(b)=[b(b-x)]^2\). With a weak-duality bound \(\Theta\) on the
  non-mirror cross sum, \(G_j\ge M\log(1/t)-K_1\), and the reduction raises
  the mirror atoms *up* to the exact face \(b_j=1\) — so the singular term
  works for us and the mean rises, preserving feasibility for free.
  Certified at \((\rho,t_0)=(1/10,1/64)\), \(\kappa\le0.393693\);
  REFUTED at \((1/10,1/32)\); \(\kappa\le2.4786\) at \((1/50,1/1024)\).
  Report `liu9-mirror-layer.json`, digest `eae6d098d5a35ce2`. Mutation tests
  9/9, including one that rejects substituting \(W_1\) for \(A_1\) at the
  discriminating configuration where the true slope is exactly \(-\beta\).
* **A units error was found and fixed (PROVED).** `gap = EHX*(Phi-1)` is exact
  wherever `EHX>0`, and `EHX(P*) = p h(x) = 0.5526667300...`. The ceiling
  `0.70046750915...` that `liu9_tube.py` prints is in \((\Phi-1)/dist^2\)
  units — `liu9_binding.py` line 906 prints `split_unit/4` as `Phi''(0)`, and
  `split_unit` is divided by `h(x)` — while every layer kappa derived from

* **The measured complement frontier is exactly the two boundary strata
  (MACHINE VERIFIED).** `uc/liu9_survivors.py` reclassified all 79 residual
  boxes: 70 are covered by the zero-face hypothesis
  \(2(1-\beta)\mu-1\ge0.11105875229...>0\) — which is precisely the
  boundary layer's own \(\Lambda_{\text{feasible}}\) — and the other 9 are

  61 boxes are coordinate-identical across the two localized runs, not nine;

* **The endpoint degeneracy was a bookkeeping artifact (PROVED).**
  `uc/LIU_H2_INGREDIENT_I.md`, confirmed independently at 120 and 160 dps and
  by my own pencil analysis. The objective's quadratic form is
  \(A|s|^2+Cq(1-q)|d|^2\) and the distance's is \(|s|^2+q(1-q)|d|^2\); the

* **Ingredient (i) is now explicit on the chart (PROVED).**
  `uc/liu9_smooth_chart.py`. Both functionals vanish to second order at the

  \(f(v)=\frac12 v^{\mathsf T}\mathrm{Hess}f(\xi)v\) *exactly* and no third

  analytic third-derivative bound on the chart — not more compute, though the

  hit again.** First, `arb.union` outward-rounds: any ball touching 0 from

  and subtracting loses the inequality to cancellation; the manifest forms

* **Cost.** Mirror production run 18.3 s; smooth chart 1.8 s; boundary layer
  26 s; survivors 0.9 s; four subagents in parallel, peak load 9.97/28. No
  interference with Campaign K, whose three slices grew from 16/15/18 MB to

  (PROVED).** `uc/liu9_qdegenerate.py`. Inner core \(|d|\le1/32\) has

  A symmetry that would have been easy to assume is REFUTED: only the

* **A centered third-order form cut the chart obstruction by 11.2x (PROVED).**
  `uc/liu9_chart_centered.py`. Each Hessian entry is bounded by its exact

  \(h'''\) is legitimate *here* precisely because the zero-support and mirror

  from \(811.29\) to \(72.20\), the certified radius rises \(8\times\) to

* **Superseded 2026-08-29.** The chart was lifted to \(1/32\) without radial
  quadrature, the exact second-order simultaneous-insertion theorem closes the

* **Still not claimed globally.** Liu's Hypothesis 2 now fails only at the
